# Progress

## Autonomous Roadmap Progress

### Phase 0: COMPLETE
- Translation entry: `src/api/routes/program-translate.ts` is feature-gated, shadow-only, and does not call `executeF1QL`.
- Translation adapters: `src/f1ql/translator.ts` supports Anthropic and OpenAI-compatible tool calls; production uses Groq.
- Surface schema/lowering: `src/f1ql/ast.ts`, `schema.ts`, `lower.ts`.
- Core/compiler/interpreters: `src/f1ql/core.ts`, `compiler.ts`, `interpreter.ts`, `executor.ts`.
- Existing budgets: `src/f1ql/limits.ts`; API route handles program metrics/logging.
- Tests: `tests/f1ql/*`, `tests/api/in-process-routes.test.ts`; local PostgreSQL is bootstrapped by `src/test/setup.ts`.
- CI: `.github/workflows/test.yml` runs typecheck, lint, unit, golden, integration, schema, and in-process API gates.
- Property testing: `fast-check` is installed; Phase 11 WP7 uses fixed-seed bounded generators with normal shrinking and bounded replay controls.

- Phase 11 Work Package 0 scheduler safety: COMPLETE. Commit `a580d5f` removes the overflowing `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` interval sentinel and implicit startup writer. Weekly auto-sync now requires exact `AUTO_SYNC=true`; startup catch-up separately requires exact `AUTO_SYNC_STARTUP_CATCH_UP=true`. The scheduler owns one bounded primary/retry/startup timer chain, rejects duplicate starts, preserves pending downstream repair after partial failure, caps retries, handles startup/Monday overlap on both failure and success, and awaits active work during shutdown. ### Phase 5 Strict Pace Eligibility-Artifact Attempt (2026-07-23)
- Resolution: FIA's Australian Race History Chart is now acquired by the fixed-URL, evidence-only `fetch:pace-v2:fia-r1-history-artifact` parser. - Promotion boundary formalized in `docs/F1QL_SOURCE_CONTRACTS.md` and `docs/F1QL_PRODUCTION_CORPUS.md`: filtered `clean_air_gap_2_0s_v1` pace is coverage-only until one authority carries all mapped per-lap eligibility semantics. F1QL currently has no separately exposed unfiltered raw-timing metric, so no factual pace golden can be added without changing product semantics.
- Selected ready 2026 round 1 (Australian Grand Prix): the retained read-only preflight has 835 serving F1QL-eligible laps. Fresh Formula 1 artifacts were byte-retained only in a mode-0700 OS-temporary directory: `TimingData.jsonStream` (5,607,988 bytes, SHA-256 `a2521be4b468f9ec4c61211558521c993269eb34c24c600e1fa3c90ebb251c8d`) and `DriverList.jsonStream` (16,531 bytes, SHA-256 `ee6c5096ab3c3f477eaf4856ed97cb9457bc0d109e26a90d4dd182df6ab57747`). URLs and r1 DriverList provenance are committed in `data/pace-v2-official-2026-coverage-matrix.json`.
- Strict proof is BLOCKED, not partially inferred. The Lap Chart has per-lap position order only; Pit Stop Summary has driver/lap/time/stop data; On Track Analysis has transponder/pit-exit/laps/pit-entry records. None supplies a per-completed-lap validity decision or numeric gap to the car ahead. `TimingData` has incremental `IntervalToPositionAhead`, `InPit`, and `PitOut` updates, but no reviewed official contract binds an interval snapshot to every completed lap or establishes it as the FastF1 gap used by `clean_air_gap_2_0s_v1`; its pit states likewise do not specify the exact completed lap bearing the F1QL in/out flags. Thus no inclusion or exclusion can be asserted as externally equal for `clean_air_flag`, `is_valid_lap`, `is_pit_lap`, `is_in_lap`, or `is_out_lap` without inventing a mapping.
- No production write, DDL, deploy, shadow-translation execution, or pace golden promotion occurred. Exact completion requirement: retain an FIA/F1 authority artifact (and its field semantics) that keys each completed lap by racing number and lap number with validity, pit/in/out, and numeric car-ahead gap values, then run the guarded read-only per-lap comparison and promote only if every record and identity mapping is equal.

### Phase 5 Round-2 Official Lap Timing Artifact Protocol (2026-07-22)
- Located the official Formula 1 historical `TimingData.jsonStream` archive for the 2026 Chinese Grand Prix race: `https://livetiming.formula1.com/static/2026/2026-03-15_Chinese_Grand_Prix/2026-03-15_Race/TimingData.jsonStream`. Mediated retrieval demonstrated that it contains individual `LapTime` and `LapNumber` records, unlike the FIA final-classification PDF. The Formula 1 results page independently identifies the event as the 13-15 March 2026 Chinese Grand Prix and publishes only fastest laps, not the individual-lap series.
- Added `fetch:pace-v2:round2-lap-timing-artifact`, an evidence-only fixed-URL collector. It validates HTTPS/host/path and the timing-stream signature, writes a new mode-0600 temporary raw artifact, hashes it, and emits individual-lap fingerprints plus deterministic raw timed-lap medians for Norris (`1`), Bortoleto (`5`), Albon (`23`), and Piastri (`81`). It has no database, fact-generation, writer, or production path. The median protocol deduplicates final `(lap_number, seconds)` values, sorts seconds, and takes the usual odd/even median.
- Provenance artifact retained: direct system-TLS acquisition at `2026-07-22T17:24:42.772Z` wrote `/var/folders/p9/gh5frnt56_l4t03p8dl_fq3m0000gn/T/pace-v2-round2-f1-timing-yxtDKI/TimingData.jsonStream` mode `0600`, 5,248,701 bytes, SHA-256 `380259ce59c9e7b5b81aa4872106e2a3ba9e476fd7129d31f27fcc45aa2b747d`. The official endpoint's response metadata was HTTP 200, ETag `"c1a273bebbbca3f16d4cd582b1e65343"`, and Last-Modified `2026-03-15T08:59:59Z`. Its UTF-8 BOM and CRLF framing are retained byte-for-byte; the parser normalizes only those framing markers in memory.
- Limitation: the artifact's `TimingData` stream contains sector updates but no completed `NumberOfLaps`/`LastLapTime.Value` pair for any of the four required racing numbers. It therefore reports `required_driver_timing_coverage: "incomplete"` and zero timed-lap entries/`null` raw medians, rather than asserting a timing value. This is a source-coverage limitation, not evidence a driver did not participate. It also lacks a reviewed durable mapping for the application's complete clean-air/pit/in/out eligibility inputs, so no `f1ql.lap_pace` validation, individual-lap comparison, raw-median, or factual pace assertion is made.
- The direct Node client was unable to consume the same source because of the BOM signature mismatch; the evidence-only collector now uses the available system TLS client with fixed official-site headers, exact URL validation, and the stream signature check. It has no database, fact-generation, writer, or production path.
- Verification: `npm run typecheck`; `npm run test:f1ql` (230 tests in 27 files); `npm run lint` (0 errors, 123 pre-existing warnings). No production access, query, deployment, or write occurred.

### Phase 4 Pace Event Artifact Diagnosis (2026-07-22)

### Phase 4 Pace V2 Eligibility Diagnosis (2026-07-21)
- Guarded production reads confirmed that rounds 2-10 contain 9,577 active-methodology race facts with non-null times and `is_valid_lap = true`, but every fact has `is_pit_lap = true`, `is_in_lap = true`, and `is_out_lap = true`; therefore the unchanged pace eligibility predicate correctly yields zero laps. Round 1 is healthy (835 standard-eligible laps).

### Phase 5 Metamorphic and Differential Tests (2026-07-21)
- Added Docker-fixture metamorphic coverage for generic classification `source`, `filter`, `sort`, and `limit`: inversion is asserted only across unique non-null positions, small limits are ordered prefixes of larger limits, empty filters are no-ops, and independent filters may be reordered without changing results. Both race and qualifying classification are covered.
- Added compiler-versus-reference differential execution for fixture-backed standings aggregate/sort/limit, event and qualifying classification (including descending `NULLS LAST`), and staged lap-pace median aggregation. All compiled SQL executes only through `executeF1QLReadOnly` in the local wrapped test database.
- Fixed the reference interpreter's classification null ordering so `NULLS FIRST`/`LAST` is independent of sort direction, matching PostgreSQL. Existing PR CI already runs `npm run test:f1ql`, so the new suite is automatically covered; no production access or deployment was performed.

- Decision: Railway logs are deployment-scoped. The automated 30-day review therefore covers only the window since the latest deployment, not a cross-deployment rolling period. The durable-sink decision remains deferred and must not be implemented without explicit instruction.

### Phase 3 Core IR Refactor (2026-07-21)
- Checkpoint complete: `scripts/snapshot-f1ql-lowering.ts` generates `tests/fixtures/f1ql-lowering-golden.json` from the real `parseF1QLProgram` and `lowerF1QL` emitter; `tests/f1ql/lowering-golden.test.ts` compares every schema-valid shadow-corpus program exactly. The generated snapshot covers standings aggregate, pace summary, pace delta, event classification, and the schema-valid unknown-identity program.
- Next: replace bespoke core `pace_aggregate`, `subtract`, `event_classification`, and fused `sort_limit` nodes with generic Source, Filter, Aggregate, Sort, Limit, Join, Compare, and Delta nodes. Update compiler/interpreter and preserve snapshot output only where surface semantics remain intentionally unchanged; document any intentional snapshot diff before accepting it.
- Completed first compositional change: fused `sort_limit` is now `limit → sort → aggregate`. The lowering golden was regenerated from the real emitter; this is an intentional internal snapshot shape change with identical compiled SQL, interpreter rows, and API results. `typecheck`, lint (0 errors), 42 F1QL tests, and 7 in-process API tests passed.
- Completed core source boundary: standings lowering now emits a core-owned `source` node rather than reusing the surface AST type. The snapshot JSON and all 42 F1QL tests remain unchanged. Next is to generalize the source/filter types for pace and classification before replacing their bespoke compiler/interpreter paths.
- Completed bounded classification milestone: the `event_classification` surface macro now lowers to core `source(event_classification) -> filter -> sort(finishing_position asc, nulls last) -> limit`; `CoreEventClassificationNode` and its opcode dispatch were removed. Compiler and reference interpreter consume the generic chain while preserving exact predicates, parameter ordering, selected columns, ordering, and limits. The lowering golden was regenerated from the real emitter; its classification entry intentionally changed shape only. Pace nodes were not refactored.
- Verification: `npm run typecheck` passed; `npm run lint` passed with 0 errors and 117 pre-existing warnings; `npm run test:f1ql` passed 42 tests; `npm run test:api:inprocess` passed 7 tests. The shadow translator remains non-executing; its injected-executor invariant remains covered by the F1QL suite.
- Completed pace lowering: `pace_summary` now emits `source(lap_pace) → filter(valid-lap eligibility) → aggregate(median by round) → aggregate(count/mean)`. `pace_delta` emits two equivalent per-round aggregates joined on `round`, then `compare → delta`. Removed `CorePaceAggregateNode`, `CoreSubtractNode`, and their compiler/interpreter opcode dispatch. The compiler retains the established SQL parameter order for summary and delta programs; reference interpretation retains shared-event and null behavior.
- Intentional regenerated-golden difference: pace summaries formerly serialized as `pace_aggregate`; pace deltas as `subtract` with two `pace_aggregate` children. They now serialize the explicit generic `source`, `filter`, `aggregate`, `join`, `compare`, and `delta` composition. Surface renderings, API rows, valid-lap exclusions, per-event median/cross-event mean, shared-event alignment, and shadow non-execution are unchanged.
- Completed generic classification consumption: compiler and reference interpreter now recursively apply core `source`, `filter`, `sort`, and `limit` nodes. Classification no longer uses a recovered macro topology or bespoke flattened-node dispatch; source-specific SQL projection remains at the source boundary. Pace retains its bounded specialized compilation path.
- Production completion: deployment `70346161-7bd7-45e6-8abe-32530ed13f73` reached `SUCCESS`. A known-driver shadow request returned `pace_summary`; the fetched five-minute Railway report contained one attempt, 100.00% success, no rejection reasons, and `pace_summary` as the operation.
- Added `validateCoreProgram` immediately after lowering and before compilation. It applies the Phase 2 source/field signature catalog to generic core operators, rejects unsupported classification sort fields, and permits only established internal staged pace aliases. The lowering golden is unchanged because lowering output did not change; its exact real-emitter comparison remains green.
- Verification: `npm run typecheck` passed; `npm run lint` passed with 0 errors and 117 pre-existing warnings; `npm run test:f1ql` passed 43 tests; `npm run test:api:inprocess` passed 7 tests.

No database writes or persistent output were made.

Fixed the migration view projection to cast the v2 value back to untyped `numeric`, preserving the existing canonical-view contract before retry.
- Second retry also rolled back cleanly on the existing `session_type varchar(5)` view contract; the v2 projection now preserves that type explicitly before the final retry.
- Final corrected migration attempt reached production but was rejected with `cannot execute CREATE TABLE in a read-only transaction`. Railway production has only `DATABASE_URL`, with no separate primary/replica variables, and that role is read-only. No DDL was applied. PARTIAL: apply `migrations/20260721_pace_correctness_v2.sql` through the Supabase SQL editor or an approved primary migration credential, then verify `laps_normalized_v2`, `f1ql.lap_pace`, and `f1ql.event_classification` before deploying pace correctness code.

### Phase 4 Pace V2 Immutable Audit Reconciliation (2026-07-21)
- Added reviewed forward-only migration `20260724_pace_v2_audit_reconciliation.sql`. It creates append-only `pace_v2_round_audit_reconciliation` evidence with one row per race session and an immutable update/delete rejection trigger. The original `pace_v2_round_audit` is never modified.
- Added a dual-flagged, loopback-refusing read-only manifest generator and explicit primary-only serializable reconciler. Both accept only `manifest_fact_fingerprint_only_v1`: race session, positive complete row count, and active methodology must exactly match the original audit and current facts, while only the original/current fact fingerprint differs. The reconciler verifies its evidence relation and trigger and does not update facts or original audits.
- Preflight now reports reconciliation relation/trigger diagnostics and accepts immutable reconciliation evidence only for that exact otherwise-valid manifest-audit mismatch. It refuses absent, altered, non-immutable, count/method/session, manifest-fingerprint, or current/original fingerprint-invalid evidence; reconciliation cannot cover another manifest defect.
- Production intentionally not run. After applying the reviewed migration through an approved primary channel, use an authorized read-only production role to generate and independently review one manifest for each affected round; then use an approved primary role to insert evidence one round at a time. Retain manifests and stdout SHA-256 evidence and rerun guarded preflight. No migration, reconciliation, production query, or deployment was performed for this change.
- Verification: `npm run typecheck` passed; `npm run lint` passed with 0 errors and 120 pre-existing warnings; `npm run test:f1ql` passed 204 tests in 21 files.

## 2026-07-18: Shadow F1QL Translation
- Decision: `/program/translate` is independently feature-gated by `F1QL_TRANSLATION_ENABLED`.
- Decision: the initial route is shadow-only and returns a validated program without calling `executeF1QL`.
- Decision: driver identities use the strict database-backed `DriverResolver`; no humanized or guessed fallback IDs are allowed.
- Decision: translation accepts only the constrained F1QL schema. Legacy intents and SQL fallbacks are prohibited.
- Fix: Anthropic translation now uses forced tool use (`emit_f1ql_program`) instead of prompt-only text JSON after a production shadow request returned non-JSON text.
- Decision: use Groq `openai/gpt-oss-20b` through the OpenAI-compatible adapter for low-cost shadow translation.
